**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. 
No direct spatial proposition applies to the specific contradiction identified. The contradiction is based on the timing and nature of the injuries rather than spatial relationships.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
The contradiction is identified through logical analysis of the events' timing rather than spatial reasoning. The autopsy report's indication of a post-mortem head injury contradicts the testimony of retaliation with a dying breath, as retaliation implies an action taken before or at the moment of death, not after.

These propositions are relevant because they directly address the direction the victim ran and the implied position of the attacker relative to the victim.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. Both Proposition 1 and Proposition 2 are applicable. According to Testimony 8, if the victim ran to the left, the attacker should be to the right (Proposition 1). However, May Testimony (Evidence 9) indicates the victim ran to the right, suggesting the attacker was to the left (Proposition 2). This discrepancy highlights a contradiction between the testimonies regarding the direction of the victim's flight and, by extension, the position of the attacker.

**Step 4:** Since applicable propositions exist, we do not need to delve into alternative spatial reasoning.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction
Prop 1 states that if the lighter is in the victim's blood, then the lighter is near the victim. This proposition could be relevant if we consider the lighter as a key piece of evidence.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction
Given that the lighter has Athena's prints on it in the victim's blood (Evidence 18), according to Prop 1, this implies the lighter was near the victim. However, the critical aspect here is not necessarily about the location (near the victim) but about the implications of the lighter being found with the victim's blood and Athena's prints. The contradiction arises when considering how the lighter, with both Athena's prints and the victim's blood, is used as evidence against Athena without clear context of how it got there or if it directly links her to the crime scene in a way that fulfills the conditions of being the culprit based solely on this evidence.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction
The spatial reasoning here involves understanding the relationship between the lighter, Athena, and the victim. The presence of Athena's prints on the lighter in the victim's blood suggests a connection between Athena and the victim, potentially at the crime scene. However, this alone does not necessarily prove Athena's guilt without considering other factors such as motive, opportunity, and direct evidence linking her to the act of murder.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format
Given the information and focusing on the requirement for a contradiction between evidence and testimony, we look for a direct conflict. Testimony 3 by Fulbright states that the lighter with Athena's prints on it is proof that she is the culprit. However, Evidence 18, while confirming the lighter has Athena's prints and the victim's blood, does not in itself prove Athena's guilt or directly link her to the crime in a conclusive manner without additional context or evidence. Thus, the contradiction lies in the interpretation and use of the lighter as definitive proof of guilt.
